2.
The Shimura variety

2.1.
Representation theoretic notation

Let
G/\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a reductive group, such
that it becomes split over some finite extension
k/\mathbb{F}_{p}
. Fix a choice
T\subset B\subset P\subset G

of maximal torus, Borel subgroup and a parabolic subgroup, with
T,B
defined over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and
P
over
k
.
Let
P=MU_{P}
be the Levi decomposition of

P
. Let
B^{-}
be the opposite Borel, uniquely determined by
B\cap B^{-}=T
.

•

Let
X^{*}(T)=\textnormal{Hom}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}(T,\mathbb{G}_{m})
and
X_{*}(T)=\textnormal{Hom}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}(\mathbb{G}_{m},T)
be the group of characters and cocharacters respectively.
Let
W=N_{G}(T)/Z(T)
be the Weyl group of
G/k
.
The Galois group
\Gamma=\textnormal{Gal}(k/\mathbb{F}_{p})
acts on them.
Let
\sigma\in\Gamma
be the Frobenius
x\mapsto x^{p}
.

•

Let
\Phi\subset X^{*}(T)
be the set of roots and
\Phi^{\vee}\subset X_{*}(T)
the set of coroots.

•

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be the Lie algebra of
G
.
For
\alpha\in\Phi
choose
x_{\alpha}\in\mathfrak{g}
so that

\{x_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\Phi\}

is a Chevalley basis for
G_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}
. Concretely,
h_{\alpha}:=[x_{\alpha},x_{-\alpha}]
acts
as the scalar
\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle
on a vector of weight
\lambda
.
Also for
\alpha,\beta\in\Phi
,
[x_{\alpha},x_{\beta}]=(c+1)x_{\alpha+\beta}
up to a sign
when
\alpha+\beta\in\Phi
,
and
c
is the greatest positive integer such that
\alpha-c\beta\in\Phi
.

•

For each
\alpha\in\Phi
fix a root morphism

u_{\alpha}:\mathbb{G}_{a}\to U_{\alpha}\subset G
over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
, determined by
the property that
du_{\alpha}(1)=x_{\alpha}
.
They satisfy

tu_{\alpha}(x)t^{-1}=u_{\alpha}(\alpha(t)x)

for all
x\in\mathbb{G}_{a}
,
t\in T
.

•

Let
\Phi^{+}\subset\Phi
be the set of positive roots, defined by the condition that
\alpha\in\Phi^{+}

if
U_{\alpha}\subset B
, and let

\Delta\subset\Phi^{+}
be a choice of simple roots.

•

For
\alpha\in\Phi
let

\phi_{\alpha}:\textnormal{SL}_{2}\to G_{k}
be the map corresponding to
u_{\alpha}
. It satisfies

\phi_{\alpha}\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}=u_{\alpha}(t),\;\;\;\phi_{\alpha}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
t&1\end{pmatrix}=u_{-\alpha}(t).

•

For
\alpha\in\Phi
let
\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi^{\vee}

be the corresponding coroot. The tuple
(X^{*},\Phi,X_{*}(T),\Phi^{\vee})
together with their natural pairings and Galois actions
is the root system of
G/\mathbb{F}_{p}
.

•

For
\alpha\in\Phi
let
s_{\alpha}\in W
be the corresponding reflection. It acts on
X^{*}(T)
as

\lambda\mapsto\lambda-\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\alpha
. Let
w_{0}\in W
be the longest element
according to the Bruhat order.

•

The parabolic
P
corresponds to a subset
I\subset\Delta
, determined by
the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{p}
containing
x_{-\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\Delta

precisely when
\alpha\in I
. We will use the notation
P=P_{I}
. Let
W_{I}=W_{M}
be the subgroup of
W

generated by
s_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in I
, or equivalently, the Weyl group of
M
. Let

W^{I}=W^{M}
be the
set of minimal length representatives of
W_{I}\backslash W
.

•

(Dot action) Let
\rho\in X^{*}(T)
be a choice of half sum of positive roots for
G_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}
. We define the
dot action of
W
on
X^{*}(T)
by
w\cdot\lambda:=w(\lambda+\rho)-\rho
.

•

(Affine Weyl group) Let
W_{\text{aff}}=p\mathbb{\mathbb{Z}}\Phi^{+}\rtimes W
be the affine Weyl group.
The dot action of
W
on
X^{*}(T)
extends to an action of
W_{\text{aff}}
where
p\mathbb{\mathbb{Z}}\Phi^{+}
acts by
translation. Then
W_{\text{aff}}
is generated by reflections
s_{\gamma,n}
for
\gamma\in\Phi
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
which are characterized by acting as

s_{\gamma,n}\cdot\lambda=\lambda+(pn-\langle\lambda+\rho,\gamma^{\vee}\rangle)\gamma
.

•

We divide
X^{*}(T)-\rho
into
\rho
-shifted alcoves, defined as the
interior of the regions defined by the hyperplanes
H_{n,\gamma}=\{\langle\lambda+\rho,\gamma^{\vee}\rangle=np\}

for
\gamma\in\Phi
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

•

We say that

\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
is
p
-small if
|\langle\lambda+\rho,\gamma^{\vee}\rangle|<p
for
all
\gamma\in\Phi
.
The affine Weyl group acts simply transitively on
\rho
-shifted alcoves
and the stabilizer of the region of
p
-small weights is the finite Weyl group
W
.

•

Let
X_{+}(T)\subset X^{*}(T)
be the set of dominant weights
\lambda
, satisfying

\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\geq 0
for every
\alpha\in\Delta
. Similarly, let
X_{M,+}(T)

be the set of
M
-dominant weights, consisting of the
\lambda
satisfying

\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\geq 0
for all
\alpha\in\Delta_{M}
.

•

For a linear algebraic group
H
over a ring
R
, let
\textnormal{Rep}_{R}(H)
be the category of algebraic representations
of
H

which are finite projective
R
-modules. It is equivalent to the category of
modules for the algebra of distributions

U(H)
which are also
Let
\textnormal{Mod}^{\textnormal{fg}}_{R}(H)
be the category of algebraic representations of
H
which are finitely generated as
R
-modules.

Since it will be a key part of this article we highlight our definition of generic weights.

Definition 2.1.1

(
1.1.1
)
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
and
\epsilon\geq 0
. We say that
\lambda

is
\epsilon
-generic with respect to
p
if
for each
\gamma\in\Phi
, there exists an integer
n_{\gamma}
such that

n_{\gamma}p+\epsilon<\langle\lambda,\gamma^{\vee}\rangle<(n_{\gamma}+1)p-\epsilon.

Similarly, for their relevance in this article, we state the definition of dual Weyl modules, irreducible algebraic
representations, and Serre weights.

Definition 2.1.2
.

Let
G/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
be a reductive group scheme, and
T\subseteq B
a choice of maximal torus and Borel defined over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Let
P\subseteq G
be a parabolic subgroup defined over some
W(k)
, for
k/\mathbb{F}_{p}
finite.
Let
M
be the Levi of
P
.

(1)

For
\lambda\in X_{M,+}(T)
let

V_{P}(\lambda):=\text{Ind}^{P}_{B}w_{0,M}\lambda=\textnormal{H}^{0}(P/B,P\times w_{0,M}\lambda/B),

where
B
acts as
b(p,v)=(pb,w_{0,M}\lambda(b)v)
. Then
V_{P}(\lambda)\in\textnormal{Rep}_{W(k)}G
. It
is naturally inflated from the induced representation
\text{Ind}^{M}_{B^{-}\cap M}\lambda
of
M
. We say that

V_{P}(\lambda)
is the
dual Weyl module
for
M
of highest weight
\lambda
.
When
P=G
we will simply write
V(\lambda)=V_{G}(\lambda)
.
In the setup of
Section

2.2
, when
P
is the Hodge parabolic we will use
the notation
W(\lambda):=V_{P}(\lambda)
.

(2)

Let
L_{M}(\lambda)\subset V_{P}(\lambda)_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}
be the socle as an
M_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}
-representation.
We will use the notation
L(\lambda):=L_{G}(\lambda)
.
The

L(\lambda)
for
\lambda\in X_{+}(T)
parametrize all irreducible representations of
G_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}

[
36
, Cor II.2.7]
.

(3)

The set of
p
-restricted weights
X_{1}(T)\subseteq X_{+}(T)
consists of those
\lambda
such that

0\leq\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle<p
for all
\alpha\in\Delta
. Given
\lambda\in X_{1}(T)

let
F(\lambda)\in\textnormal{Rep}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}G(\mathbb{F}_{p})
be the restriction of
L(\lambda)
to
G(\mathbb{F}_{p})
.
Then
\{F(\lambda):\lambda\in X_{1}(T)\}
contains all the
Serre weights
, i.e. irreducible representations in

\textnormal{Rep}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}G(\mathbb{F}_{p})
. Moreover,
F(\lambda)=F(\lambda^{\prime})
only if
\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\in(\sigma-\text{id})X^{0}(T):=(\sigma-\text{id})\{\lambda\in X^{*}(T):\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle=0\text{ for all }\alpha\in\Delta\}

[
21
, Lem 9.2.4]
.

2.2.
The integral model

We recall some of the properties of the integral model of our Shimura variety.
Consider the Siegel Shimura datum

(\textnormal{GSp}_{2g},S^{\pm})
. We take
\textnormal{GSp}_{2g}/\mathbb{Q}
to be the base change to
\mathbb{Q}

of the reductive group over
\mathbb{Z}

defined as the group of symplectic similitudes of the free abelian group
\Lambda
of rank
2g
together with a
symplectic form
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
. We choose a basis
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{2g}\}
for
\Lambda

so that
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle

is given by the matrix

J=\begin{pmatrix}0&S\\
-S&0\end{pmatrix}

with
S
the matrix with
1
s on the antidiagonal and
0
s elsewhere. Denote by

0\to L\to\Lambda\to L^{\vee}\to 0

the exact sequence where
L=\langle e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{g}\rangle
is maximal isotropic for
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
.
The Siegel double space
S^{\pm}

is defined as the set of maps
h:\mathbb{S}\to\textnormal{GSp}_{2g,\mathbb{R}}
such that

h
induces a Hodge structure of weights
(-1,0)
,
(0,-1)
on
\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}
, and
the pairing
(x,y)\mapsto\langle x,h(i)y\rangle
is positive or negative definite on
\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum of Hodge type, that is, we fix an embedding of Shimura data

i:(G,X)\hookrightarrow(\textnormal{GSp}_{2g},S^{\pm})
for some
g\geq 1
. For us,
(G,X)
satisfies axioms
(SV1,2,3) in Deligne’s definition
[
58
, §6]

[
14
]
, with SV3 saying that
G^{\text{ad}}
has no
\mathbb{Q}
-simple factor such
that
h
is trivial on it.
Let
E_{0}/\mathbb{Q}
be the reflex field of
(G,X)
.
Fix a prime
p
, and a prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
E_{0}
above
p
.
Let
E
be the completion of
E_{0}
at the prime
\mathfrak{p}
, with
ring of integers
\mathcal{O}
and residue field
k
.
Assume that there is a reductive model
G_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
whose generic fiber is
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, and fix such a choice.
Let
K\subseteq G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})

be a compact open neat subgroup of the form
K=K_{p}K^{p}
with
K_{p}=G_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, i.e. it is a level hyperspecial
at
p
. By the existence of
G_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
we have that
p
is unramified in
E_{0}
, since
G_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
will be
split over
W(k^{\prime})
for some
k^{\prime}/\mathbb{F}_{p}
finite. Therefore,
\mathcal{O}=W(k)
.
After possibly replacing
\Lambda
by another lattice in
\Lambda\otimes\mathbb{Q}
, there exist tensors
s_{\alpha}\in\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\otimes}
such that
G_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\subset\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)
is the group of transformations leaving
s_{\alpha}

invariant
[
44
, Lem 2.3.1]
. For a module
M
we will use
M^{\otimes}
to denote the direct sum of all the combinations of tensor powers, duals,
symmetric powers, and wedge powers of
M
. In particular, the symplectic pairing restricts to a pairing

\Lambda\times\Lambda\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

Let
K^{\prime}_{p}\subseteq\textnormal{GSp}(\Lambda)(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be the stabilizer of
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. Then we can choose a level

K^{\prime}=K^{{}^{\prime}p}K^{{}^{\prime}}_{p}\subseteq\textnormal{GSp}(\Lambda)(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})
such that there is a closed embedding of Shimura varieties

[
44
, Lem 2.1.2]

\textnormal{Sh}_{K}(G,X)\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(\textnormal{GSp},S^{\pm})_{E}.

Let
\textnormal{Sh}_{\textnormal{GSp},K^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}

be the canonical integral model for the Shimura variety for
(\textnormal{GSp}_{2g},S^{\pm})
. Then Kisin
[
44
]
,
Vasiu
[
71
]
, and
[
43
]
(for
p=2
)
construct a canonical smooth integral model
\textnormal{Sh}_{K}/\mathcal{O}
of
\textnormal{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
together with a closed embedding

i:\textnormal{Sh}_{K}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Sh}_{\textnormal{GSp},K^{\prime},\mathcal{O}}
.
It is defined as the flat closure of
\textnormal{Sh}_{K,E}\to\textnormal{Sh}_{K^{\prime},\textnormal{GSp},\mathcal{O}}
. In
[
44
]

one also takes the normalization, but
[
74
]
proved
that this was not necessary.
Let
\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}:=\textnormal{Sh}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}
be the geometric special fiber. With the integral model defined, we can state our definition of generic weights for a statement involving the
Shimura variety.

Definition 2.2.1
.

(
1.1.1
)
Fix a Shimura datum
(G,X)
of Hodge type. We fix
a maximal torus and a Borel
T\subseteq B\subseteq G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
.
Let
X=\{X_{p,K}\}_{p,K}
be a family of statements ranging over all rational primes
p
and levels
K\subseteq G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})

which are hyperspecial at
p
, concerning

\textnormal{Sh}_{K,\mathcal{O}}
(where
\mathcal{O}
lives over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
) and some weights
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
.
We say that
X
holds for
generic

\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
if there exists
\epsilon\geq 0
, depending only on

G_{\mathbb{Q}}
, such that each statement
X_{p,K}
holds for weights
\lambda
which are
\epsilon
-generic with respect to
p
.

Associated to
h
there is a conjugacy class of cocharacters

\mu_{0}:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to G
defined over
E
. Since
G_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
is quasi-split we can choose
B\subseteq G_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}

such that both its generic and special fibers are Borel subgroups.
Choose a maximal torus
T\subseteq B_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
, it lifts to a torus
T
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Then
there is an associated cocharacter
\tilde{\mu}:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to G
defined over
\mathcal{O}
as in
[
77
, 2.2.3]
whose base change to
E
lies in
the conjugacy class of
\mu_{0}
. There exists a unique parabolic
P\subseteq G_{\mathcal{O}}

such that
B\subseteq P
and such that it is
the parabolic associated to a
conjugate of
\tilde{\mu}\in X_{*}(T)
. Namely,
P=P_{\mu}:=\{g\in G:\lim_{t\to 0}\mu(t)g\mu(t^{-1})\;\;\text{exists}\}
,
where
\mu\in X_{*}(T)
is the dominant conjugate of
\tilde{\mu}
.
We will fix such a tuple
(T,B,P,G)
with all of them defined over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
except
P
, which is defined over
\mathcal{O}
.

2.2.1.
Hodge embeddings

We will need some structural results on what the possible Hodge embeddings are, since that will restrict what
G_{\mathbb{Q}}

can be. The most important result in this subsection is
Lemma

2.2.6
ahead,
which will be used repeatedly.

Definition 2.2.2
.

We say that a
connected semisimple group over a field
k
is almost-simple if every proper normal algebraic subgroup is finite.
Given a semisimple group
G
over
k
, let
\{G_{i}:i\in I\}
be the set of almost-simple normal subgroups of
G_{\overline{k}}
.
Then there is an isogeny

\prod G_{i}\to G_{\overline{k}}
. Given
i\in I
we define
G^{i}
as the cokernel of
\prod_{j\neq i}G_{j}\to G_{\overline{k}}
,
it is isogenous to
G_{i}
.
We will refer to
G_{i}
as the almost-simple factors, and to

G^{i}
as the almost-simple quotients.
Then
\textnormal{Gal}(\overline{k}/k)
permutes the
G_{i}
, and thus also the
G^{i}
.

Lemma 2.2.3
.

Let
G
be a reductive group over a field
k
, and
G^{\textnormal{der}}\to\textnormal{GL}(V)
a representation over
\overline{k}
.
Then
we can extend
V
to a representation of
G_{\overline{k}}
.

Proof.

We have that
G_{\overline{k}}=\mu\backslash(T\times G^{\text{der}})
where
T
is a split torus and
\mu=Z(G)\cap G^{\textnormal{der}}\subseteq T
is
a finite
subgroup scheme. Thus, to extend
V
we just need to extend the character by which
\mu
acts on
V
to
T
.
We have that
\mu
decomposes into a product

\mu=\prod\mu_{n_{i}}\subseteq\prod\mathbb{G}_{m}=T
, with
\mu_{n_{i}}
the multiplicative group of order
n_{i}
.
It is easy to see that a character of
\mu_{n_{i}}
extends to a character of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
∎

Given
G
a reductive group, we say that a cocharacter
\mu
is minuscule if

\lvert\langle\mu,\gamma\rangle\rvert\leq 1

for every root
\gamma\in\Phi
.
For a minuscule cocharacter
\mu
of
G
, we say that
\mu
is non-trivial on an almost-simple factor
G_{i}
of

G^{\text{der}}
if there exists a simple root
\alpha
of
G_{i}
such that
\langle\mu,\alpha\rangle=\pm 1
.
If moreover
\mu
is dominant, i.e.
\langle\mu,\alpha\rangle\geq 0
for all simple roots
\alpha
,
then there exists a unique
\alpha\in\Delta_{G_{i}}
such that
\langle\mu,\alpha\rangle=1
, and
we say that it is the
special node
for
\mu
and
G_{i}
.
When talking about the special node for a conjugacy class of cocharacters we will
do it with respect to a dominant representative
\mu
in the conjugacy class.
We will use the notation of
4.2.4
for the roots of the different semisimple Lie algebras.
We recall that we are assuming that our Shimura variety satisfies the axiom SV3.

Proposition 2.2.4
.

Consider the Hodge embedding
G_{\mathbb{C}}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)_{\mathbb{C}}
.
The image of
G
contains the scalars, and the associated cocharacter on
\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)

is conjugate to one of the standard minuscule cocharacters. Also,
G^{\textnormal{der}}
does not contain
an exceptional group as an almost-simple factor.
Further,
G^{\textnormal{der}}_{\mathbb{C}}
is a quotient by a finite group of a product
\prod H_{i}
, which we now describe.
The action of
H_{i}
on
\Lambda
is a direct sum of minuscule representations
\Lambda_{i}
.
Moreover,
H_{i}
is one of the following.

•

(Type
A
)
H_{i}
is simply connected of type
A_{n}
for
n\geq 1
.

(1)

The special node of
\mu
for
H_{i}
can be any simple root.
Assume that the special node for
\mu
and
H_{i}
is neither
\alpha_{1}
nor
\alpha_{n}
. Then

\Lambda_{i}
can only be the standard representation
\textnormal{SL}_{n+1}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{GL}_{n+1}
.

(2)

If the special node for
\mu
is one of the above, then
\Lambda_{i}
can be any minuscule representation, corresponding
to the wedge powers of the standard representation.

•

(Type
B
)
H_{i}
is simply connected of type
B_{n}
for
n\geq 2
.
Then the special node of
\mu
for
H_{i}
must be
\alpha_{1}
.
There is only one option for
\Lambda_{i}
, corresponding to the spin representation.

•

(Type
C
)
H_{i}
is simply connected of type
C_{n}
for
n\geq 2
. The special node
for
\mu
must be
\alpha_{n}
.
There is only one minuscule representation
\Lambda_{i}
, which is the
standard representation
\textnormal{Sp}_{2n}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{GL}_{2n}
.

•

(Type
D
)
H_{i}
is of type
D_{n}
for
n\geq 4
.
There are two options. Either
H_{i}=\textnormal{SO}_{2n}
is of type
D_{n}
for
n\geq 5
,
with special node
\alpha_{n-1}
or
\alpha_{n}
, in which case
\Lambda_{i}
can only be
the standard representation. Or
H_{i}
is simply connected of type
D_{n}
, in which case

\Lambda_{i}
can be either of the two half-spin representations.
For
n\geq 5
this implies
that the special node is
\alpha_{1}
, and for
n=4
either one of
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{n-1},\alpha_{n}\}

can be the special node.

Proof.

Let
H
be the simply connected cover of
G^{\textnormal{der}}
. Then each almost-simple factor
H^{\text{sc}}_{i}

of
H
admits the complexification of a

symplectic representation
with finite kernel as in
[
58
, Def 10.2]
via the Hodge embedding.
Fix some
i
and assume first that
\mu
is non-trivial on it, and that
H^{\text{sc}}_{i}
is contained in the
complexification of a factor of non-compact
type of
H_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then under these conditions, in
[
58
, Summary 10.8]
and the classification right before, they
describe the possibilities for what the special node for
H^{\text{sc}}_{i}
and
\Lambda_{i}

can be, as in the statement.
To deduce the same properties about the action of
H^{\text{sc}}_{i}
on
\Lambda
for all
i
,
we use the
\textnormal{Gal}_{\mathbb{Q}}
action on the almost-simple factors,
and their subrepresentations of
\Lambda
.
Since we are assuming that our Shimura variety satisfies axioms SV1,2,3, we have that
each
\mathbb{Q}
-simple factor of
G^{\textnormal{der}}
has a non-compact factor over
\mathbb{R}
.
Similarly, by SV3 each
\mathbb{Q}
-simple factor of
H
contains
an almost-simple factor
such that
\mu
is non-trivial on it, so we deduce that each
H^{\text{sc}}_{i}
acts
on
\Lambda
as in the statement. Therefore, if
\mu
is non-trivial on
H_{i}
its special node must be as stated.
Only in type
D_{n}
with
n\geq 5
and special node
\alpha_{n-1}
or
\alpha_{n}
it must happen that
\Lambda_{i}

factors through
\textnormal{SO}_{2n}
.
Therefore,
G^{\textnormal{der}}
is the quotient of
\prod H_{i}
by a finite group.
∎

Definition 2.2.5
.

Let
G
be a reductive group over a field, and
Q\subseteq G
a parabolic subgroup. It is defined by a cocharacter

\mu\in X_{*}(G)
in the sense that

Q=\{g\in G:\lim_{t\to 0}\mu(t)g\mu(t^{-1})\;\;\text{exists}\}
. For a representation
\rho:G\to\textnormal{GL}(V)

we say that the parabolic
Q_{V,\mu}
associated to
(Q,\mu)
is the
one which is induced by
\rho\circ\mu
.
In the case that
Q=P
is the Hodge parabolic, there is a unique
\mu
in the conjugacy class of the Hodge
cocharacter lying in
X_{*}(T)
such that
P
is associated to
\mu
,
so we will omit
\mu
from the notation. Similarly, over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
, for
Q=P^{-(p)}
we take its cocharacter to be

-\sigma\mu
.

In order to reduce some results to the case of
\textnormal{GL}_{n}
we will need the following important lemma.
We will use it
repeatedly in
Section

3.3
, and in the subsequent results that depend on it.
Recall that
G
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
P
over
k
.
Let
P^{(p)}=P\times_{k,\sigma}k
denote the base change along the
p
th power Frobenius
\sigma
.
We consider it sitting inside
G
via the base change map
P^{(p)}\to P
. For
z\in W
, let
B^{z}=\dot{z}B\dot{z}^{-1}

for some representative
\dot{z}\in N(T)
of
z
.
Before proceeding with the statement, let us give some motivation.
In
Section

3.3
we will see that there exists a map
\mathcal{F}l\to[B\times B^{z}\backslash G]
. The lemma below induces
a map
[B\times B^{z}\backslash G]\hookrightarrow[B_{0}\times B^{z^{\prime}}_{0}\backslash\textnormal{GL}(V)]
sending the open cell
to the open cell, so that we can relate the Hasse invariants on both sides. Moreover, we will need
B_{0}\subseteq P_{V}

to make sense of a Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism for
\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}l}
.

Lemma 2.2.6
.

Fix a Borel pair
(T,B)
of
G_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}

such that
B\subseteq P
.
Let
z\in W
be such that
B^{z}
over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
is the Borel generated by
U_{P^{-(p)}}
and
(B\cap M)^{(p)}

[
24
, Lem 2.3.4]
.
Let
V\in\textnormal{Rep}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}(G)
be an embedding of the form
V=\oplus V_{j}
such that each weight space of
V_{j}
is
1
-dimensional. There exists a Borel pair
(T_{0},B_{0})
of

\textnormal{GL}(V)_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}
and
z^{\prime}\in W_{\textnormal{GL}(V)}

such that under the map
G\hookrightarrow\textnormal{GL}(V)
we have
B\subseteq B_{0}\subseteq P_{V}
,

B^{z}\subseteq B^{z^{\prime}}_{0}

and
Bw_{0}z^{-1}B^{z}\subseteq B_{0}w_{0,\textnormal{GL}}z^{{}^{\prime}-1}B^{z^{\prime}}_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
\mu
be a dominant representative of the Hodge
cocharacter. Choose some basis
\{v_{i}:i\in I\}
of
V
such that each
v_{i}

lies in a single weight space of
V
. This defines a maximal torus
T_{0}\subseteq\textnormal{GL}(V)

such that
T\subseteq T_{0}
. Define
B_{0}

by an ordering
\{v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{n}\}
of the basis above (with
v_{1}
generating the rank
1
object) via the following procedure.
First choose
v_{1}
to be a highest weight vector such that
\langle\mu,w(v_{1})\rangle
is
maximal among all the basis vectors, where
w(v_{i})
denotes the weight of
v_{i}
.
This is possible since
\mu
is dominant.
Let
i\geq 1
and suppose that
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{i}\}
have been chosen with the following properties:

\langle\mu,w(v_{1})\rangle\geq\langle\mu,w(v_{2})\rangle\geq\ldots\langle\mu,w(v_{i})\rangle
,
and no pair with
(1\leq j<k\leq i)

exists such that
w(v_{k})>w(v_{j})
.
To choose
v_{i+1}
, let
S\subset I
consist of the
j\in I\setminus\{1,2,\ldots,i\}
such
that
w(v_{j})
is maximal under the
\geq
order. Choose
v_{i+1}
to be
v_{j}
for some
j\in S
such that

\langle\mu,w(v_{j})\rangle
is maximal among the elements of
S
.
Then we cannot have
w(v_{i+1})>w(v_{k})
for some
1\leq k<i+1
by the way

v_{k}
was chosen, and
\langle\mu,w(v_{i})\rangle\geq\langle\mu,w(v_{i+1})\rangle
by the way
v_{i}
was chosen and since
\mu
is dominant.
Therefore, we have constructed
\{v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{n}\}
such that if
w(v_{i})\geq w(v_{j})
then
j\geq i
, and

\langle\mu,w(v_{i})\rangle\geq\langle\mu,w(v_{i+1})\rangle
. The second condition is equivalent to

B_{0}\subseteq P_{V}
. The first one implies that
B\subseteq B_{0}
. This can be checked at the level of Lie algebras.
For
\alpha\in\Phi^{+}

we have that
x_{\alpha}v_{i}\in\langle v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{i}\rangle
by the way the set
\{v_{j}\}
is defined.
Since the
x_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\Phi^{+}
generate
\textnormal{Lie}(B)
,
this means that
\textnormal{Lie}(B)\subseteq\textnormal{Lie}(B_{0})
as desired. The choice of
(T,T_{0})
induces a map

W\to W_{\textnormal{GL}(V)}
. This is because the weight spaces of each
V_{j}\subseteq V
are
1
-dimensional, so that
W
permutes the

1
-dimensional subspaces given by the basis
\{v_{i}\}
.
Let
z^{\prime}=zw_{0}w_{0,\textnormal{GL}}\in W_{\textnormal{GL}(V)}
under this map.
Proving
B^{z}\subseteq B^{z^{\prime}}_{0}
is equivalent to proving the inclusion of opposite Borels
B^{-}\subseteq B^{-}_{0}
,
which follows in the same way as
B\subseteq B_{0}
does. Then since
w_{0}z^{-1}
maps to
w_{0,\textnormal{GL}}z^{{}^{\prime}-1}\in W_{\textnormal{GL}(V)}

we obtain that
Bw_{0}z^{-1}B^{z}\subseteq B_{0}w_{0,\textnormal{GL}}z^{{}^{\prime}-1}B^{z^{\prime}}_{0}
.
∎

We note that an irreducible minuscule representation
V
satisfies that all of its weight spaces are
1
-dimensional,
so that
Lemma

2.2.6
applies to the Hodge embedding.
By essentially the same proof we get the following result integrally. Let
\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}=W(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
.

Lemma 2.2.7
.

Let
\rho:G\hookrightarrow\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)
be the Hodge embedding over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
There exists tuple
T_{0}\subseteq B_{0}\subseteq P_{0}\subseteq\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)

defined over
\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}

of a maximal torus, Borel and
P_{0}=P_{\rho\circ\mu}
the parabolic associated to
\rho\circ\mu

such that

G\cap(T_{0},B_{0},P_{0},\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda))=(T,B,P,G)_{\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
under the Hodge embedding.

Proof.

We construct
T_{0},B_{0}
over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
as in
Lemma

2.2.6
and then we lift them
to
\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
∎

When working over
\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
we will also fix
a tuple

(T_{0},B_{0},P_{0},\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda))
with the property that
G\cap(T_{0},B_{0},P_{0},\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda))=(T,B,P,G)_{\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
.

2.2.2.
Automorphic vector bundles

Recall that we have fixed an embedding of
the tuples
(T,B,P,G)\hookrightarrow(T_{0},B_{0},P_{0},\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)=\textnormal{GL}_{n})
, with all the members of the first
tuple defined over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}

except
P
, and the second tuple defined over
\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. We will invoke
Lemma

2.2.6
to choose another embedding and tuple
over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
whenever it is needed.
Let
0\to L\to\Lambda\to L^{\vee}\to 0
be the filtration on
\Lambda
induced by
P_{0}
.
Similarly,
B_{0}
defines a full descending flag
F^{\bullet}_{\Lambda}\subseteq\Lambda_{\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
compatible
with the Hodge filtration.
Let
\{v_{i}\}
be a basis of
\Lambda_{\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
such that
v_{i}
generates
F^{n-i}_{\Lambda}/F^{n-i+1}_{\Lambda}
.
Let
h:A\to\textnormal{Sh}
be the pullback of the universal abelian variety
over
\textnormal{Sh}_{\textnormal{GSp}}
, and let
\mathcal{H}:=\textnormal{H}^{1}_{\textnormal{dR}}(A/\textnormal{Sh})
. It is a vector bundle equipped with the Gauss–Manin
connection
\nabla
. In general, the Gauss–Manin connection is defined for a triple
X/Y/T
of schemes with

Y/T
smooth,

\nabla:\textnormal{H}^{i}_{\textnormal{dR}}(X/Y)\to\textnormal{H}^{i}_{\textnormal{dR}}(X/Y)\otimes\Omega^{1}_{Y/T}

[
40
]
.
It comes by pullback from the same object
\mathcal{H}_{\textnormal{GSp}}
over
\textnormal{Sh}_{\textnormal{GSp}}
. From the degeneration
of the Hodge de Rham spectral sequence it is equipped with a Hodge filtration

0\to\omega\to\mathcal{H}\to\omega^{\vee}_{A^{\vee}}\to 0

where
\omega=e^{*}\Omega^{1}_{A/\textnormal{Sh}}
, with
e:\textnormal{Sh}\to A
the zero section. Kisin

[
44
, Cor 2.3.9]
proves that there exist some natural tensors

s_{\alpha,\textnormal{dR}}\in\mathcal{H}^{\otimes}
such that on every
\mathcal{O}
-point of
Sh

they define the group
G_{\mathcal{O}}
, and on
\mathbb{C}
-points
they are naturally identified with the tensors induced by
s_{\alpha}
under the complex de-Rham-Betti comparison
theorem
[
44
, §2.2]
. They are moreover horizontal for
\nabla
.

Definition 2.2.8
.

Let

G_{\textnormal{dR}}=\text{Isom}_{\textnormal{Sh}}((\mathcal{H},s_{\alpha,\textnormal{dR}}),(\Lambda,s_{\alpha})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{Sh}}).

It is an étale (left)
G
-torsor over
Sh
, with
G
acting on
\Lambda

[
77
, Thm 2.4.1]
.
Similarly, let

P_{\textnormal{dR}}=\text{Isom}_{\textnormal{Sh}}((\omega\subseteq\mathcal{H},s_{\alpha,\textnormal{dR}}),(L\subseteq\Lambda,s_{\alpha})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{Sh}}).

It is an étale
P
-torsor.

As an auxiliary space we can define the flag Shimura variety.

Definition 2.2.9
.

Let
\mathcal{F}l:=P_{\textnormal{dR}}/B
, it comes with a map
\pi:\mathcal{F}l\to\textnormal{Sh}
, whose fibers are isomorphic to
P/B
.
Let
B_{\textnormal{dR}}
be
{P}_{\textnormal{dR}}\to\mathcal{F}l
considered as a
B
-torsor over
\mathcal{F}l
.

There is a
P
-equivariant embedding
B_{\textnormal{dR}}\hookrightarrow P_{\textnormal{dR}}\times_{\textnormal{Sh}}\mathcal{F}l
over
\mathcal{F}l
, and in fact

B_{\textnormal{dR}}\times^{B}P\cong P_{\textnormal{dR}}\times_{\textnormal{Sh}}\mathcal{F}l
as
P
-torsors over
\mathcal{F}l
.
Consider the embedding of pairs
(G,B)\to(\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda),B_{0})

over
\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
Then the pushout
B_{\textnormal{dR}}\times^{B}B_{0}\subseteq G_{\textnormal{dR}}\times^{G}\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)
defines a descending filtration
F^{i}_{\mathcal{H}}\subset\mathcal{H}
, such
that
\mathcal{L}_{i}:=\text{gr}^{i}F^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{H}}
are line bundles. It is easy to see that if

F^{\bullet}_{\Lambda}\subseteq\Lambda_{\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
is the flag induced by
B_{0}
, then
since
B_{\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}=G\cap B_{0}

B_{\textnormal{dR},\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}=\text{Isom}_{\mathcal{F}l}((F^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{H}},s_{\alpha,\textnormal{dR}}),(F^{\bullet}_{\Lambda},s_{\alpha})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}l}).

Definition 2.2.10
.

(Automorphic vector bundles)
The torsors above define exact functors

F_{P}:\textnormal{Rep}_{\mathcal{O}}(P)\to\text{Coh}(\textnormal{Sh}),\;\;\;F_{B}:\textnormal{Rep}_{\mathcal{O}}(B)\to\text{Coh}(\mathcal{F}l),\;\;F_{G}:\textnormal{Rep}_{\mathcal{O}}(G)\to\text{Coh}(\textnormal{Sh})

by e.g.
F_{P}(V)=P_{\textnormal{dR}}\times^{P}V
, which by definition locally consists of pairs

(\phi,v)
with
\phi\in P_{\textnormal{dR}},v\in V\otimes\mathcal{O}

up to the equivalence relation
(\phi,v)=(p\phi,pv)
for
p\in P
. These are compatible with the base change map
\mathcal{O}\to k

on both sides,
and the functors naturally extend to
representations which are a filtered colimit of finitely generated representations.
Similarly, we can define by pushout
F_{\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)}
,
F_{P_{0}}
and
F_{B_{0}}
. When the domain
is clear we will also use the notation
\mathcal{V}:=F_{B}(V)
. We define

\mathcal{L}(\lambda):=F_{B}(\lambda)
as a line bundle over
\mathcal{F}l
,
and
\omega(\lambda):=F_{P}(W(\lambda))
, where
W(\lambda)
is the dual Weyl module for
M
as in

2.1.2
.

For
V\in\textnormal{Rep}(P)
, when considered as a
B
-representation we have that
\pi^{*}F_{P}(V)=F_{B}(V)
, since

B_{\textnormal{dR}}\times^{B}P\cong P_{\textnormal{dR}}\times_{\textnormal{Sh}}\mathcal{F}l
.

Lemma 2.2.11
.

We have
\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}(\lambda)=\omega(w_{0,M}\lambda)
.

Proof.

This follows as in
[
1
, Prop 3.20]
using that
\text{ind}^{P}_{B}\lambda=\text{ind}^{M}_{B\cap M}\lambda=\text{ind}^{M}_{B^{-}\cap M}w_{0,M}\lambda=W(w_{0,M}\lambda)
, since
(B\cap M)^{w_{0,M}}:=w_{0,M}(B\cap M)w^{-1}_{0,M}=B^{-}\cap M
.
∎

The next proposition shows that automorphic vector bundles coming from
G
-representations
also carry a flat connection. The proof follows

[
60
, §5.2.1]
, but we record it here since we will use its construction often.

Proposition 2.2.12
.

Let
R\in\{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}/p^{n}\}
, and let
V\in\textnormal{Mod}^{\textnormal{fg}}_{R}(P)
with a compatible action of
\mathfrak{g}
, i.e.
the derivative of
P
(well-defined since
V
is finitely generated) and the action of
\mathfrak{p}=\textnormal{Lie}(P)
agree.
Then
F_{P}(V)
is equipped with a natural flat connection over
\textnormal{Sh}_{R}
.
The same property holds replacing
P
by
B
.

Proof.

Let
\phi\in P_{\textnormal{dR}}
be a local section. By transporting the Gauss–Manin connection along
\phi
we get a connection

\tilde{\nabla}_{\phi}:\Lambda\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{Sh}}\to\Lambda\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\textnormal{Sh}}
. The restriction
to
\Lambda
is
\mathcal{O}
-linear, which can be seen as an element

\xi_{\phi}\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\textnormal{Sh}}
, since
the tensors
s_{\alpha,\textnormal{dR}}
are parallel for
\nabla
and
\phi
maps them to
s_{\alpha}
.
The image of
\xi_{\phi}
under
\mathfrak{g}\otimes_{R}\Omega^{1}_{\textnormal{Sh}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{V}\otimes\text{id}}\text{End}(V)\otimes_{R}\Omega^{1}_{\textnormal{Sh}}
, where
\rho_{V}
is the
\mathfrak{g}
action on
V
, can be extended
to a connection on
V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\textnormal{Sh}}
in a unique way, which we denote by
\nabla_{\phi}
.
Define a connection
\nabla:F_{P}(V)\to F_{P}(V)\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\textnormal{Sh}}=P_{\textnormal{dR}}\times^{P}(V\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\textnormal{Sh}})
,
where
in the second equality
P
acts trivially on
\Omega^{1}_{\textnormal{Sh}}
, by

\nabla(\phi,v)=(\phi,\nabla_{\phi}(v)).

Checking the compatibility with the
P
action reduces to the identity
p^{-1}\circ\nabla_{p\phi}\circ p=\nabla_{\phi}

for all
p\in P
.
From the definition of
\nabla_{\phi}

it follows from the identity
\xi_{p\phi}=p\xi_{\phi}p^{-1}
, which can be easily checked.
It is clear that

\nabla
is a flat connection
since each
\nabla_{\phi}
is.
∎

Remark 2.2.13
.

Consider the following setup. Let
P\subseteq G
be a parabolic inside a reductive group,
E/X
is a vector bundle
with a flat connection
\nabla
over a scheme
X
,
I_{G}
is a
G
-torsor on
X
consisting of trivializations of
E

respecting some tensors of
E
that are horizontal for
\nabla
, and
I_{P}\subseteq I_{G}
a sub
P
-torsor.
Then for
V
a
(U\mathfrak{g},P)
-module as above, the proof above shows that

I_{P}\times^{P}V
is equipped with a flat connection.

2.3.
Examples

We list a few examples of Hodge type Shimura varieties which can serve to illustrate our general setup. We will be use some of them in
Section

7
.

Example 2.3.1
.

(PEL Shimura varieties)
The Shimura varieties that we will use for applications are of PEL type, which
have the advantage of having an integral model which has a moduli interpretation. The data to define
a PEL Shimura variety (over a number field) is a tuple
(D,*,\Lambda,\langle,\rangle,h)
where

(D,*)
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra with a positive involution
*
,
\Lambda
is a finitely generated
D
-module
equipped with a non-degenerate
\mathbb{Q}
-valued alternating form
\langle,\rangle
such that

\langle bv,w\rangle=\langle v,b^{*}w\rangle
for all
b\in D
,
v,w\in\Lambda
. This induces an involution
on the algebra
C=\text{End}_{D}(\Lambda)
. Finally,
h:\mathbb{C}\to C_{\mathbb{R}}
is a
*
-homomorphism
such that the symmetric bilinear form
\langle\cdot,h(i)\cdot\rangle
on
\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}
is positive-definite.
Then the group
G
associated to the data is defined by
G(R):=\{(x,\nu)\in C_{R}\times R^{\times}:xx^{*}=\nu\}
.
We say that
\nu:G\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
is the similitude character. One can then define a
Shimura datum
(G,X)

and a moduli problem of PEL type defined over the reflex field. Given a rational PEL data
as above a choice of integral PEL data consists
of a tuple
(\mathcal{O}_{D},*,\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}},\langle,\rangle)
where
\mathcal{O}_{D}
is an order on
D
stable
under
*
and
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}
is a lattice in
\Lambda
stable by
\mathcal{O}_{D}
, and such that
\langle,\rangle

takes integer values on
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then an integral PEL datum defines an integral
PEL moduli problem, and hence an integral model for the Shimura variety.
We will use this to specify the integral model of a PEL
Shimura variety, where we will assume that
p
is a good prime for the integral PEL data, as in
[
9
, Def 2.1.6]
.
We refer to
[
47
]
for more details.

Example 2.3.2
.

(Compact unitary Shimura varieties)
For applications it can be convenient to consider compact unitary varieties. Let
E_{0}/\mathbb{Q}
be quadratic imaginary and
n,a,b
as before,
and let
B/E_{0}
a division algebra of dimension
n^{2}
over
E_{0}
such
that
E_{0}
is its center, admits a positive involution
*
of the second kind
(
z^{*}=z^{c}
for all
z\in E_{0}
), and
B
splits over the primes above
p
. Let
\mathfrak{p}
be such a prime,
and
E:=E_{0\mathfrak{p}}
.
Then
B_{\mathfrak{p}}\cong M_{n}(E)
, so we let
\mathcal{O}_{B}/\mathcal{O}_{E_{0}}
the maximal order stable under

*
such that

\mathcal{O}_{B,\mathfrak{p}}=M_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{E})
.
Let
\epsilon\in\mathcal{O}_{B}
be the idempotent corresponding to the matrix with a
1
in the entry
(1,1)

and zeros elsewhere. Finally, let
\Lambda=B/E_{0}
as a module over itself together with a non-degenerate

*
-hermitian alternating form
\langle,\rangle
such that
\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}
has signature
(a,b)
.
We choose as a lattice
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}=\mathcal{O}_{B}
.
Let
Sh
be the PEL Shimura variety associated to the integral data

(\mathcal{O}_{B},*,\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}},\langle,\rangle)
.
The integral model is defined over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
if
a\neq b
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
otherwise.
It is
proper smooth.
Applying the idempotent
\epsilon
to the
\sigma
-isotypic component of
the Hodge filtration of
A/\textnormal{Sh}
(in the case that
p
is split in
E
one can also consider
\epsilon

for the prime corresponding to
\sigma
) we get

0\to\omega_{\epsilon}\to\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}\to\omega^{\vee}_{A^{\vee},\epsilon}\to 0,

where the first term has rank
a
and the last
b
. We can identify
\omega^{\vee}_{A^{\vee},\epsilon}

with
\omega^{\vee}_{\epsilon}
. This defines
P_{\textnormal{dR}}
, we will use
the notation
\omega(\lambda)=\omega_{\epsilon}(\lambda_{a})\otimes\omega^{\vee}_{\epsilon}(\lambda_{b})
.
We have THAT
G_{\mathbb{Q}}=GU(a,b)
with respect to
E/\mathbb{Q}
. If
p
splits in
E
we have
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}=\textnormal{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
and if
p
is
inert
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}=GU(n)
with respect to
\mathbb{Q}_{p^{2}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
In
2.2.4
the relevant Hodge embedding is

\textnormal{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{GL}_{2n}
, sending
(A,v)
to the block diagonal matrix given by
(A,vA^{-1,t})
.
For more details see
[
57
, §2.1]
. When the signature is
(n-1,1)
these are also referred to as
Harris–Taylor Shimura varieties,
[
29
]
.

Example 2.3.3
.

(Spin Shimura varieties)
To get examples of Shimura varieties where
G
is of type
B
or
D
we introduce the following example.
It is also an example of a Hodge type Shimura variety which is not of PEL type.
For more details see
[
55
]
.
Let
n\geq 1
, and
p>2
. Let
V
be a finite dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space of dimension
n+2

with a bilinear form
B
whose quadratic form
Q
is non-degenerate of signature
(n,2)
over
\mathbb{R}
.
Let
L\subseteq V\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
lattice with such that
B
is a perfect form on it, i.e.
it induces an isomorphism
L\cong L^{\vee}
.
Let
C
be its Clifford algebra, defined to be the universal algebra over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with a map

L\to C
that satisfies

v^{2}=Q(v)
for
v\in C
. By construction it has a
\mathbb{Z}/2
-grading
C=C^{+}\oplus C^{-}
.
Then
G=\text{GSpin}(L)=\{x\in(C^{+})^{\times}:xLx^{-1}=L\}
. The action by conjugation of
C
on
L

defines the standard representation
G\hookrightarrow\textnormal{GL}(L)
.
Under these conditions
one can define a symplectic form
\psi
on

C
such that it admits a Shimura datum of Hodge type
(G,X)\hookrightarrow(\textnormal{GSp}(C,\psi),S^{\pm})
.
The reflex field is
\mathbb{Q}
,
\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}
has dimension
n
and
G_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}=\text{GSpin}_{n+2}
,
which for
n
even it is of type
D
and for
n
odd it is of type
B
. In the language of
2.2.4

the special node corresponding to
\mu
is
\alpha_{1}
, which means that the induced Hodge filtration on the standard
representation is a
2
-step filtration of ranks
1,n+1,n+2
. The Hodge embedding is the spin representation,
which for type
B
it is irreducible, and for type
D
it is the sum of the two half-spin representations.

2.4.
Hecke operators away from
p

Fix a level
K=K^{p}K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{\mathbb{Q}})
, and let
\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p}

be the finite adeles away from
p
. Consider
\mathcal{H}^{p}=C^{\infty}_{c}(G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p})//K^{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, the abstract Hecke algebra away from
p

defined as a convolution algebra of locally constant, compactly supported,
bi-
K^{p}
invariant functions on
G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p})
with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
We define the action of
\mathcal{H}^{p}
on coherent cohomology via Hecke correspondences.
Let
g\in G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p})
, and
K_{g}=K\cap gKg^{-1}
. There are two finite étale maps

p_{1},p_{2}:\text{Sh}_{K_{g}}\to\text{Sh}_{K}
over
\mathcal{O}
defined as follows. On the generic fiber

p_{1}
is defined by the inclusion
K_{g}\subseteq K
, and
p_{2}
is defined
by composing
\text{Sh}_{K_{g}}\to\text{Sh}_{gKg^{-1}}
defined as in
p_{1}
with the isomorphism

[g]:\text{Sh}_{gKg^{-1}}\to\text{Sh}_{K}
, defined on
\text{Sh}_{gKg^{-1}}(\mathbb{C})=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X\times G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})/gKg^{-1}
by
(x,h)\mapsto(x,hg)
.
By
[
44
, Lem 2.1.2]
we can also find a level
K^{\prime}
of
\textnormal{GSp}_{2g}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})
hyperspecial at
p

such that it fits in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Sh}_{K_{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{g^{\prime}},\textnormal{GSp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Sh}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Sh}_{K^{\prime},\textnormal{GSp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{i}}

over the generic fiber, where
g^{\prime}\in\textnormal{GSp}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p})
is the image of
g
.
We can define both projections integrally on
\textnormal{Sh}_{\textnormal{GSp}}
as follows.
The points of the Siegel Shimura variety are tuples
(A,\lambda,\eta_{K})
of an abelian variety

A
with prime-to-
p
polarization
\lambda
and level structure
\eta_{K}
as in

[
50
, Def 1.3.8]
. Then
p_{1}
is defined by

(A,\lambda,\eta_{K_{g}})\to(A,\lambda,\eta_{K})
where
\eta_{K}
is obtained by taking a
K
-orbit of any

\eta
in
\eta_{K_{g}}
, and
[g]
is defined by sending
A
to the unique

A^{\prime}
prime to
p
quasi-isogenous to
A
via
f:A\to A^{\prime}
satisfying the conditions in

[
50
, Prop 1.4.3.4]
. By functoriality of flat closure we get (unique) maps

p_{i}:\textnormal{Sh}_{K_{g}}\to\textnormal{Sh}_{K}
over
\mathcal{O}
which can be used to define the square above integrally.
Moreover, these projections are finite étale.
We can check that this correspondence only depends on the coset
K^{p}gK^{p}

(by checking it both in the Siegel case and on the generic fiber), so in particular
one can check that
it induces
an action of
\mathcal{H}^{p}
by correspondences, and it readily extends to
\mathcal{F}l_{K}
.

We claim that for
Q\in\{G,P\}
, we have natural
isomorphisms of torsors
T_{g}:p^{*}_{1}Q_{\textnormal{dR}}\cong p^{*}_{2}Q_{\textnormal{dR}}
on
Sh
or
\mathcal{F}l
. Over

\textnormal{Sh}_{\textnormal{GSp}}
this is induced by the map
f:A\to A^{\prime}
used to define
[g^{\prime}]
. By definition of
Q_{\textnormal{dR}}

we just need to check that the isomorphism

\textnormal{H}^{1}_{\textnormal{dR}}(A/\textnormal{Sh})\cong\textnormal{H}^{1}_{\textnormal{dR}}(A^{\prime}/\textnormal{Sh})
respects the de Rham tensors, which can
be checked on formal completions of points, by the construction of the tensors
[
44
, 1.5.4]
.
This induces an action in cohomology: for
V\in\textnormal{Rep}_{\mathcal{O}}(P)

T_{g}:\textnormal{H}^{i}(\text{Sh}_{K},F_{P}(V))\xrightarrow{p^{*}_{2}}\textnormal{H}^{i}(\text{Sh}_{K_{g}},p^{*}_{2}F_{P}(V))\xrightarrow{T_{g}}\textnormal{H}^{i}(\text{Sh}_{K_{g}},p^{*}_{1}F_{P}(V))\xrightarrow{\text{Tr }p_{1}}\textnormal{H}^{i}(\text{Sh}_{K},F_{P}(V)),

and similarly over
\mathcal{F}l
.
We say that a collection of maps
F_{Q}(V)_{K}\to F_{Q}(W)_{K}

of automorphic vector bundles, ranging across
K=K^{p}K_{p}
neat with
K_{p}
hyperspecial
is Hecke equivariant away from
p
if the associated maps on cohomology are equivariant for
\mathcal{H}^{p}
.
From the definition of
T_{g}
it is enough to check that the maps are compatible with base change and
prime to
p
quasi-isogenies
f:A\to A^{\prime}
, in the
sense that they are compatible under replacing the torsors
Q_{\textnormal{dR}}
with the ones defined by
\textnormal{H}^{1}_{\textnormal{dR}}(A^{\prime}/\text{Sh}_{K})
. Similarly,
there is an action of
\mathcal{H}^{p}
on de Rham cohomology of
Sh
with coefficients in
\mathcal{V}
for

V\in\textnormal{Rep}(G)
. This is because the isomorphisms
T_{g}:p^{*}_{1}G_{\textnormal{dR}}\cong p^{*}_{2}G_{\textnormal{dR}}
are compatible with the way the Gauss–Manin connection is defined on

\mathcal{V}
, since the projections
p_{i}
are étale.

Given a level
K
let
S
be a finite set of places containing
p

such that
G(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
is contained in
K
(for some reductive model over
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
) for
\ell\notin S
.
Let
\mathbb{T}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
be the spherical Hecke algebra
away from
S
, defined as a restricted tensor product of local Hecke algebras

\bigotimes^{{}^{\prime}}_{\ell\notin S}C^{\infty}_{c}(G(\mathbb{Q}_{l})//G(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}),\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
It is a commutative subalgebra of
\mathcal{H}^{p}
, so it also acts on coherent cohomology.
Although it depends on the level
K
we will suppress it from the notation.

2.5.
Toroidal compactifications

Many Shimura varieties are not proper, so we introduce the machinery of toroidal compactifications, with
the aim of extending all our operators to them. Here
\textnormal{Sh}/\mathcal{O}
is always considered with respect to
K_{p}

hyperspecial.

Theorem 2.5.1
.

Let
\Sigma
be a complete smooth admissible rational polyhedral cone decomposition
with respect to
(G,X,K)
as in
[
56
, §2.1.23]
.
By
[
56
]
there exists an associated toroidal compactification

\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor},\Sigma}
over
\mathcal{O}
. It is a smooth
proper scheme satisfying the following properties.

(1)

The boundary
D\coloneqq\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor},\Sigma}-\textnormal{Sh}
with its reduced structure
is a Cartier divisor with simple normal crossings.

(2)

The universal abelian scheme on
Sh

extends to a semi-abelian scheme
\pi^{\textnormal{tor}}:A^{\textnormal{tor}}\to\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}
.
The prime to
p
polarization extends to a prime to
p
isogeny

\lambda:A^{\textnormal{tor}}\to A^{\textnormal{tor},\vee}
.
Define
\omega^{\textnormal{tor}}=e^{*}\Omega^{1}_{A^{\textnormal{tor}}/\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}}
, it extends
\omega/\textnormal{Sh}
.
In characteristic
p
the Frobenius and Verschiebung maps extend to
\omega^{\textnormal{tor}}
.

(3)

There is a canonical extension
\mathcal{H}^{\textnormal{tor}}
of
\mathcal{H}
to a vector bundle over
\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}
in such a way
the de Rham tensors extend to
\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}
, and we can define
an extension of the
G
torsor
G_{\textnormal{dR}}
to
\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}

[
56
, Prop 4.3.7]
.
Moreover, it fits in the exact sequence

0\to\omega^{\textnormal{tor}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\textnormal{tor}}\to\omega^{\textnormal{tor},\vee}_{A^{\vee}}\to 0

which can be used to define an extension of the
P
torsor
P_{\textnormal{dR}}

[
56
, Prop 4.3.9]
.

(4)

We extend
\mathcal{F}l\to\textnormal{Sh}
to
\pi^{\textnormal{tor}}:\mathcal{F}l^{\textnormal{tor}}\to\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}
by defining it to be

P_{\textnormal{dR}}/B
.
Let
D_{\mathcal{F}l}=\pi^{\textnormal{tor},-1}(D)
be its boundary divisor. The functors defining automorphic vector bundles
extend to
F_{B}^{\textnormal{can}}:\textnormal{Rep}_{R}(B)\to\textnormal{Coh}(\mathcal{F}l^{\textnormal{tor}}_{R})
using
B_{\textnormal{dR}}
over
\mathcal{F}l^{\textnormal{tor}}
.
We define subcanonical extensions by
F^{\textnormal{sub}}_{B}(V)=F^{\textnormal{can}}_{B}(V)(-D_{\mathcal{F}l})
, and similarly for
\textnormal{Rep}(P)
. This defines extensions
\mathcal{L}^{?}(\lambda)
,
\omega^{?}(\lambda)
for

?\in\{\textnormal{can},\textnormal{sub}\}
,
and we still have
\pi^{\textnormal{tor}}_{*}\mathcal{L}^{?}(\lambda)=\omega^{?}(w_{0,M}\lambda)
.
From now on we will largely drop the indices for canonical
extensions.

(5)

For some appropriate hyperspecial level the Hodge embedding extends to a closed embedding

\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}_{\textnormal{GSp}}
.

(6)

There exists a log connection

\nabla^{\textnormal{tor}}:\mathcal{H}^{\textnormal{tor}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\textnormal{tor}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\log D)
extending
the Gauss–Manin connection, and the de Rham tensors in
(3)
are parallel under it. This
induces log connections on both
F^{\textnormal{can}}_{G}(V)
and
F^{\textnormal{sub}}_{G}(V)
for
V\in\textnormal{Rep}(G)
.

(7)

The coherent cohomology groups
\textnormal{H}^{i}(\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor},\Sigma},\omega^{?}(\lambda))
for
?\in\{\textnormal{can},\textnormal{sub}\}

are independent of the cone decomposition
\Sigma
.

(8)

In the PEL setting when the codimension of
Sh
in its minimal compactification is at least
2
we have

\textnormal{H}^{0}(\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}_{R},\omega(\lambda))=\textnormal{H}^{0}(\textnormal{Sh}_{R},\omega(\lambda))
for
R\in\{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{F}_{p}\}
, by

[
51
]
. This is satisfied when
Sh
decomposes up to finite morphisms
as a product of factors which are
either proper or of dimension at least
2
over
\mathcal{O}
.

(9)

The Hecke operators away from
p

extend to operators

T_{g}:\textnormal{H}^{i}(\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}},\omega^{?}(\lambda))\to\textnormal{H}^{i}(\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}},\omega^{?}(\lambda))

for
?\in\{\textnormal{sub},\textnormal{can}\}
.

2.6.
Deformation theory and the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism

Here we explain a version of the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism that works for the connection attached
to any
V\in\textnormal{Rep}(G)
, and
we upgrade the isomorphism to
\mathcal{F}l
. This is proved using the deformation theory of the integral model,
as studied in
[
44
]
.
Let
x\in\textnormal{Sh}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
. Let
\mathbb{X}
be the pullback of the
p
-divisible group
A[p^{\infty}]

to
\mathcal{O}^{\wedge}_{\textnormal{Sh},x}
, and
\mathbb{X}_{0}
the reduction over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}=\kappa(x)
. Let
W=W(k(x))
.
Let
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{X}_{0})(W)=(M^{1}_{0}\subseteq M_{0},\phi_{M_{0}})
be the filtered Dieudonne module of
\mathbb{X}_{0}
.
Then Faltings and Kisin
[
44
, Prop 2.3.5]

prove that
\mathcal{O}^{\wedge}_{\textnormal{Sh},x}\cong R_{G}
, where the latter
is the ring of functions of the completion at the identity of the opposite unipotent
U^{-}_{P,W}
.
This is a quotient of
R
,
the versal deformation ring of
\mathbb{X}_{0}
. Then
M:=\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{X})(R_{G})=M_{0}\otimes R_{G}

with its filtration
M^{1}
being induced by
M_{0}
, and the Frobenius is given by the universal element in
U^{-}_{P}(R_{G})
.
We can also identify
M
with the pullback
\mathcal{H}_{R_{G}}
.
By virtue of being a crystal it is also equipped with a flat topologically nilpotent connection
\nabla_{R_{G}}

with the property that the Frobenius
\phi^{*}M\to M
is horizontal for it.

Lemma 2.6.1
.

The connection
\nabla_{R_{G}}
is the pullback connection from the Gauss–Manin connection on
\mathcal{H}
.

Let
(G,P)\hookrightarrow(\textnormal{GL}_{n},P_{0})
be induced by the Hodge embedding. Let
U^{-}_{\textnormal{GL}}
be the opposite unipotent
to
P_{0}
. We can identify
\mathfrak{gl}_{n}/\mathfrak{p}_{0}
with
\textnormal{Hom}_{W}(L,L^{\vee})
.

Proposition 2.6.2
.

(Kodaira–Spencer on formal completions)
There exists a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{R_{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{P}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{R}\otimes R_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{P_{0}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{n}/\mathfrak{p}_{0})\otimes R_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{KS}_{R_{G}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{KS}_{\textnormal{GL},R}\otimes R_{G}}

here
\textnormal{KS}_{\textnormal{GL},R}
is defined by
D\mapsto\nabla_{R,D}\in\textnormal{Hom}(M^{1},M/M^{1})
. The two horizontal maps
are isomorphisms.

Proof.

Since on both formal completions the Hodge filtration is induced from the one on
M
we have identifications

F_{P_{0}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{n}/\mathfrak{p}_{0})=\text{Lie}(U^{-}_{\textnormal{GL}})\otimes R
, and
F_{P}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})=\text{Lie}(U^{-}_{P})\otimes R_{G}
. The fact that
\textnormal{KS}_{\textnormal{GL},R}
is an isomorphism
is a classical fact, since
R
is the versal deformation ring of
\mathbb{X}_{0}
. Moreover,

[
44
, §1.5]

shows that
\nabla_{R}
restricted to
M_{0}
is given by an element
\omega
of
\textnormal{Lie}(U^{-}_{\textnormal{GL}})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{R}
.
Since
\nabla_{R_{G}}
is the pullback connection, and the tensors
s_{\alpha,\textnormal{dR}}
are parallel under it, this shows
that the restriction of
\nabla_{R_{G}}
to
M_{0}
is given by an element
in
\textnormal{Lie}(U^{-}_{P})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{R_{G}}
, which is also the projection of
\omega\otimes 1
via

\Omega^{1}_{R}\otimes R_{G}\to\Omega^{1}_{R_{G}}
.
This shows that
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{G}}
exists, and it fits in the diagram.
It is automatically injective, and the cokernels of the vertical inclusions are locally free, since
R\to R_{G}

is induced from
U^{-}_{P}\hookrightarrow U^{-}_{\textnormal{GL}}
and both of them are smooth.
After choosing compatible splittings we see that
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{G}}
must be an isomorphism, since
\textnormal{KS}_{\textnormal{GL},R}

is given by a block diagonal matrix.
∎

We will also need a more general version of the previous proposition.

Proposition 2.6.3
.

Let
V\in\textnormal{Rep}_{W}(G)
such that
\textnormal{Lie}(G^{\textnormal{der}})\to\textnormal{Lie}(\textnormal{SL}(V))
is an embedding with a free quotient. Then
P_{V}
from
2.2.5
defines a descending filtration

F^{\bullet}_{V}
of
V
. We identify
\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{p}_{V}
with

\oplus_{k}\textnormal{Hom}(F^{k}_{V},V/F^{k}_{V})
. Define
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{G},V}:T_{R_{G}}\to F_{P_{V}}(\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{p}_{V})

by
D\mapsto\nabla_{D}
. It fits in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{P}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{R_{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{P_{V}}(\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{p}_{V}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{KS}_{R_{G},V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{KS}_{R_{G}}}

so in particular the diagonal is an isomorphism independent of
V
.

Proof.

We follow
[
55
, Prop 4.16]
. Note that the vertical embedding only depends on the Lie algebra of
G^{\textnormal{der}}
.
First we prove that the diagonal arrow exists. Since the cokernel of vertical
arrow is locally free we can prove the factorization after tensoring with
R_{G}\to W
. We can identify

T_{R_{G}}\otimes W
with
\textnormal{Lie}(U^{-}_{P})\otimes W
, then it is enough to show that

\textnormal{KS}_{R_{G},V}\otimes W
corresponds to the natural map

\textnormal{Lie}(U^{-}_{P})\to\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{p}_{V}
.
For
V=\Lambda
this can be extracted from the proof of
2.6.2
.
For a general
V
the construction of the Gauss–Manin
connection on
V\otimes R_{G}
in
Remark

2.2.13
goes by applying

\nabla_{\Lambda}\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{R_{G}}
to
V
,
so we can reduce to the case
V=\Lambda
.
We prove that the diagonal map is independent of
V
, so that it must be
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{G}}
. For
V_{1},V_{2}\in\textnormal{Rep}_{W}(G)

consider
V=V_{1}\oplus V_{2}
, and we can see that both
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{G},V_{i}}
factor through

F_{P_{V}}(\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{p}_{V})
.
∎

The same proposition as above applies to
V\in\textnormal{Rep}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}(G)
and the corresponding special fibers.
From these local results we get a description of
T_{\textnormal{Sh}}
.

Proposition 2.6.4
.

(Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism)
Let
V\in\textnormal{Rep}_{\mathcal{O}}(G)
such that the differential
\textnormal{Lie}(G^{\textnormal{der}})\to\textnormal{Lie}(\textnormal{SL}(V))
is an embedding
with a free quotient.
Define
\textnormal{KS}_{\textnormal{GL}(V)}
to be the linear map
T_{\textnormal{Sh}}\to F_{P_{0}}(\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{p}_{V})

given by
D\mapsto(x\in F^{k}_{\mathcal{V}}\mapsto\nabla_{D}(x)\bmod F^{k}_{\mathcal{V}})
. It factors through
a Hecke equivariant away from
p
isomorphism

\textnormal{KS}:T_{\textnormal{Sh}}\cong F_{P}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p}),

which is moreover independent of the choice of
V
. We can always take

V
to be the Hodge embedding. Using the log Gauss–Manin connection over
\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}
we can
define
\textnormal{KS}_{\textnormal{GL}(V)}:T_{\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}}(-\log D)\to F_{P_{0}}(\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{p}_{V})
. It factors through
a Hecke equivariant away from
p
isomorphism

\textnormal{KS}:T_{\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}}(-\log D)\cong F_{P}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p}).

Proof.

It is enough to check it on formal completions of
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-points, where it follows from
2.6.2
,

Lemma

2.6.1
and
2.6.3
. We prove the Hecke equivariance.
The map
\textnormal{KS}_{\textnormal{GL}(V)}

is Hecke equivariant since the Hodge filtration and the Gauss–Manin connection are compatible with base change
and prime-to-
p
isogenies. Since
KS
is fully determined by
\textnormal{KS}_{\textnormal{GL}(V)}
it is also Hecke equivariant.
For the statement over
\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}
, the existence of
KS
can be checked on a dense open subset, since
the cokernel of
F_{P}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})\to F_{P_{V}}(\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{p}_{V})
is a vector bundle,
and each connected component of
Sh
is integral. The fact that it is an isomorphism holds in the Siegel case by

[
18
]
, and since we can choose the toroidal compactifications compatible with the Hodge embedding

i:\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}_{\textnormal{GSp}}

we get that
KS
is injective, hence an isomorphism, since
the cokernel of
T_{\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}}(-\log D)\hookrightarrow i^{*}T_{\textnormal{Sh}^{\textnormal{tor}}_{\textnormal{GSp}}}(-\log D^{\prime})
is locally free. It is Hecke equivariant
since the log Gauss–Manin connection is.
∎

We can upgrade this isomorphism to one for
T_{\mathcal{F}l}
.
For
V\in\textnormal{Rep}_{\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(G)
choose a Borel
B\subseteq B_{0}\subseteq P_{V}\subseteq\textnormal{GL}(V)
.
That is, it is given by a full flag
F^{\bullet}_{B,V}
refining the one induced by
P_{V}
.
We identify
\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{b}_{0}
with a system of compatible elements
\phi_{i}\in\textnormal{Hom}(F^{i}_{B,V},V/F^{i}_{B,V})
.
We first note that the Kodaira–Spencer map is also an isomorphism on
G/B
. Let

F_{G/B}:\textnormal{Rep}(B)\to\text{Coh}(G/B)
be the functor defined by the canonical
B
-torsor over

G/B
.

Lemma 2.6.5
.

Let
(B,G)
be a Borel and a reductive group.
The map
\textnormal{KS}:T_{G/B}\to F_{G/B}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{b})
defined by
D\mapsto(\nabla_{D}\in\textnormal{Hom}(F^{i}_{\Lambda}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G/B},\Lambda\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G/B}/F^{i}_{\Lambda}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G/B})
is an isomorphism.

We can prove a local Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism for
T_{\mathcal{F}l}
.
Let
y\in\mathcal{F}l(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
mapping to
x\in\textnormal{Sh}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
. Then

\mathcal{F}l^{\wedge}_{y}\to\textnormal{Sh}^{\wedge}_{x}
is identified with

U^{-\wedge}_{B,\text{id}}\to U^{-\wedge}_{P,\text{id}}
. On functions write it as

R_{G}\to R_{B}
.
Let
(B_{0},P_{\Lambda},\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda))
be a choice of Borel contained in
P_{0}=P_{\Lambda}
.
Write
R\to R_{B_{0}}
,
where
R_{B_{0}}=P_{0,\textnormal{dR},R}/B_{0}
.

Proposition 2.6.6
.

(1)

There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{R_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{B}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{b})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{R_{B_{0}}\otimes R_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{B_{0}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{n}/\mathfrak{b}_{0})\otimes R_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{KS}_{R_{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{KS}_{R_{B_{0}}}\otimes R_{B}}

where both horizontal maps are isomorphisms, and
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{B_{0}}}
is defined by

D\mapsto\nabla_{R_{B_{0}},D}

seen as a compatible family of maps
\textnormal{Hom}_{R_{B_{0}}}(F^{i}_{\Lambda}\otimes R_{B_{0}},M_{R_{B_{0}}}/F^{i}_{\Lambda}\otimes R_{B_{0}})
.

(2)

Moreover,

\textnormal{KS}_{R_{B}}
maps the exact sequence
0\to T_{R_{B}/R_{G}}\to T_{R_{B}}\to\pi^{*}T_{R_{G}}\to 0

to
0\to F_{B}(\mathfrak{p}/\mathfrak{b})\to F_{B}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{b})\to F_{B}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})\to 0
,
where the quotient map is
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{G}}
.

(3)

More generally, let
V\in\textnormal{Rep}_{W}(G)
as in
2.6.3
. Choose a Borel
B_{0}\subseteq\textnormal{GL}(V)

such that
B\subseteq B_{0}\subseteq P_{V}\subseteq\textnormal{GL}(V)
. Then the natural map

T_{R_{B}}\to F_{B_{0}}(\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{b}_{0})\otimes R_{B}
factors through

F_{B}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{b})

via
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{B}}
.

Proof.

The map
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{B_{0}}}
is well-defined by the property of being a connection. After fixing
a trivialization of
M_{0}
, we have that
R_{B_{0}}=R\times P_{0}/B_{0}
, and
R_{B}=R_{G}\times P/B
.
We claim that
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{B_{0}}}
maps the exact sequence

T_{R_{B_{0}}/R}\to T_{R_{B_{0}}}\to\pi^{*}T_{R}
to the one induced by
\mathfrak{p}_{0}/\mathfrak{b}_{0}\to\mathfrak{gl}_{n}/\mathfrak{b}_{0}\to\mathfrak{gl}_{n}/\mathfrak{p}_{0}
. This is because for
D\in T_{R_{B_{0}}/R}
,

\nabla_{D}(\omega)\subseteq\omega

by functoriality of
\nabla
along
R\to R_{B_{0}}
, so
D
maps to zero
in
F_{P_{0}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{0}/\mathfrak{p_{0}})
. The two extremes
of the sequences are mapped isomorphically by
2.6.2

and
Lemma

2.6.5
with respect to
P/B=M/(M\cap B)
, so that
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{B_{0}}}
is an isomorphism.
As in the proof of
2.6.2
, the restriction of
\nabla_{R_{B_{0}}}
to
M_{0}

is given by an element of
\text{Lie}(U^{-}_{B_{0}})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{R_{B_{0}}}
.
By preservation of the tensors,
\nabla_{R_{B}}

is then given by an element of
\text{Lie}(U^{-}_{B})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{R_{B}}
,
which proves that
\textnormal{KS}_{R_{B}}

is well-defined, and by smoothness it is an isomorphism. Part
(2)

follows from the statement for
\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)
. Finally, for
(3)
, the map

T_{R_{B}}\to F_{B_{0}}(\mathfrak{gl}(V)/\mathfrak{b}_{0})\otimes R_{B}
also satisfies the analogous
property to
(2)
, by functoriality of the connection, and that
B_{0}\subseteq P_{V}
. Both graded pieces of
the map are isomorphisms, by
2.6.3
and
Lemma

2.6.5
. Independence of
V
follows
as in the proof of
2.6.3
.

∎

Globally we get the following result describing
T_{\mathcal{F}l}
.

Proposition 2.6.7
.

Let
V\in\textnormal{Rep}_{\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(G)
as in
2.6.3
. Choose a Borel
B_{0}\subseteq\textnormal{GL}(V)

such that
B\subseteq B_{0}\subseteq P_{V}\subseteq\textnormal{GL}(V)
.
There exists a commutative diagram

\hbox to155.58pt{\vbox to57.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 77.78897pt\lower-27.0472pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

where
e
is defined by sending
D\in T_{\mathcal{F}l}
to the compatible family induced by
\nabla_{D}

on
F^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{V}}
.
Then
e^{1}_{1}
is a Hecke equivariant away from
p
isomorphism, it is independent of
V
, and it
fits in the diagram of exact sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{B}(\mathfrak{p}/\mathfrak{b})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{B}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{b})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{B}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\mathcal{F}l/\textnormal{Sh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\mathcal{F}l/\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}T_{\textnormal{Sh}/\check{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{1}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}\textnormal{KS}}

Moreover,
e^{1}_{1}
extends to a
Hecke equivariant away from
p
isomorphism
e^{1}_{1}:T_{\mathcal{F}l^{\textnormal{tor}}}(-\log D)\cong F_{B}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{b})
.

Proof.

We can check all the required properties about
e^{1}_{1}
on formal completions of points,
where it follows from
2.6.6
.
The extension to
\mathcal{F}l^{\textnormal{tor}}
follows immediately from the extension of the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism.

∎

2.7.
The Grothendieck–Messing period map

We reinterpret the isomorphisms of
2.6.7
on divided power formal
completions of points in terms of the Grothendieck–Messing period map. This will be crucially used
in
Section

3
.
First recall an important property of crystals.

Lemma 2.7.1
.

[
70
,
Tag 07J6
]

Let
k/\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a perfect field,
R\to k
a PD thickening, and
W=W(k)
. Let
\mathbb{X}_{0}

be a
p
-divisible group over
k
. Then
M=\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{X}_{0})(R)
comes equipped with a crystalline connection

\nabla_{M}:M\to M\otimes\Omega^{1}_{R/W,\delta}
. There is a map of
PD pairs
(W,k)\to(R,k)
induced by the natural section of
R/p\to k
. By the crystal property
it induces an isomorphism

M\cong\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{X}_{0})(W)\otimes R
. Then any element
m\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{X}_{0})(W)

satisfies
\nabla_{M}(m\otimes 1)=0
.

Let
y\in\mathcal{F}l(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})

and
x\in\textnormal{Sh}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
its projection. Let
\mathbb{X}_{0}
be the
p
-divisible over
\kappa(y)
coming from the universal abelian variety,
and
M_{0}=\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{X}_{0})(W)
.
Choose a trivialization

\phi
of
(F^{\bullet}_{0}\subseteq M_{0},s_{\alpha,\textnormal{dR},y})
over
W
such
that over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
it identifies
F^{\bullet}_{0}
with
\infty=[B]\in G/B
. Let
\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}
be the divided power
formal completion
at
x
, and
let
\mathcal{F}l^{\sharp}_{y}
be the divided power
formal completion of
\mathcal{F}l^{\wedge}_{y}
along the ideal defined by
x
. Observe that in the notation of
Section

2.6

\mathcal{F}l^{\wedge}_{y}

is the pullback of
\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}\to\text{Spf}(R^{\sharp})
along
R^{\sharp}_{B_{0}}:=P_{0,\textnormal{dR},R^{\sharp}}/B_{0}
. Here
R^{\sharp}
is the PD completion of
R
along
x
.
Then
R^{\sharp}_{B_{0}}
parametrizes full flags
M^{\bullet}\subseteq M_{R^{\sharp}}
containing
M^{1}
.
Let

I_{P}=[G\to G/P],I_{B}=[G\to G/B]
be the tautological
P
and
B
-torsors over the respective
flag varieties. They induce functors
F_{G/P}:\textnormal{Rep}(P)\to\text{Coh}(G/P)
and

F_{G/B}:\textnormal{Rep}(B)\to\text{Coh}(G/B)
. After pulling back to the formal completion
G/P^{\wedge}_{\infty}

we make the identification

I_{P}=[G^{\wedge}_{P}\to G/P^{\wedge}_{\infty}]
.

Proposition 2.7.2
.

Fix a trivialization
\phi
as above. There exist Grothendieck–Messing period maps (depending on
\phi
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G/P^{\wedge}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}l^{\sharp}_{y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G/B^{\wedge}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Spf}R^{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textnormal{GL}_{n}/P_{0}}^{\wedge}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Spf}R^{\sharp}_{B_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textnormal{GL}_{n}/B_{0}}^{\wedge}_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{P_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{B_{0}}}

with

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}l^{\sharp}_{y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G/B^{\wedge}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G/P^{\wedge}_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{P}}

being cartesian. They satisfy the following properties.

(1)

There are natural isomorphisms
\pi_{P}^{*}I_{P}\cong P_{\textnormal{dR}}
and

\pi_{B}^{*}I_{B}\cong B_{\textnormal{dR}}
.

(2)

The connection
\nabla_{\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}}
on
\mathcal{H}

is the pullback connection along
\pi_{P}
of the
trivial connection on
F_{G/P}(\Lambda)=\Lambda\otimes\mathcal{O}_{G/P}
.
The analogous statement holds for
B
.

Proof.

The definition of
\pi_{P_{0}}
is as follows. It is enough to define it on points
(R\to k)

of
\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}

that are PD thickenings. By the crystal property

(2.7.1)

\mathcal{H}_{R}\cong\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{X}_{0})(R)\cong M_{0}\otimes R\cong\Lambda\otimes R,

where the last isomorphism is given by
\phi
. Therefore,
it induces a trivialization of the torsor
\textnormal{GL}_{\textnormal{dR}}
over
R
.
The map
\pi_{P_{0}}
sends
R
to
P_{0,\textnormal{dR},R}\subseteq\textnormal{GL}_{\textnormal{dR},R}\cong\textnormal{GL}_{R}
, which defines
a point of
G/P^{\wedge}_{0,\infty}(R)
by the choice of
\phi
, and since
the second isomorphism respects the Hodge filtration over
k
.
Similarly, the second isomorphism in (
2.7.1
) is compatible with the tensors
s_{\alpha,\textnormal{dR}}
and
s_{\alpha}

by construction of
s_{\alpha,\textnormal{dR}}

[
44
, 1.5.4]
,
so that it induces a trivialization of
G_{\textnormal{dR},R}
, and
\pi
is defined by

P_{\textnormal{dR},R}\subseteq G_{\textnormal{dR},R}\cong G_{R}
. They clearly fit in the commutative diagram.
For
(1)
, as a space over
\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}
we have

\pi_{P}^{*}I_{P}(R\to k)=\{y\in\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}(R),g\in G^{\wedge}_{P}(R):\pi_{P}(y)=[gP\subseteq G]\}

with
P
acting on
g
on the right.
We define a map
\pi_{P}^{*}I_{P}\cong P_{\textnormal{dR}}
by sending
(y,g)
to the element of
P_{\textnormal{dR},R}
corresponding to
g

under
gP\cong P_{\textnormal{dR},R}
. It is clearly
P
-equivariant. Conversely, an element of
P_{\textnormal{dR},R}
defines
an element of
G(R)
via
P_{\textnormal{dR},R}\subseteq G_{R}
. It factors through
G^{\wedge}_{P}(R)
since
G_{\textnormal{dR},R}\cong G_{R}

respects the Hodge filtration over
k
.
For
(2)
, by definition
\pi^{*}F_{G/P}(\Lambda)(R)=\mathcal{H}_{R}\cong M_{0}\otimes R
,
and the pullback connection is defined by
\nabla(m\otimes 1)=0
for
m\in M_{0}
.
By
Lemma

2.7.1
this agrees with the Gauss–Manin connection
\nabla_{R}
on
\mathcal{H}_{R}
.
Doing this for every
R

ensures the equality over
\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}
.
The definition of
\pi_{B_{0}}
is the same but using
B_{0,\textnormal{dR}}
instead of
P_{0,\textnormal{dR}}
, and by the same reasoning

\pi_{B}
is well-defined and fits in the diagram. The cartesianness of the diagram involving
\pi_{P}

and
\pi_{B}
follows since
[G/B^{\wedge}_{\infty}\to G/P^{\wedge}_{\infty}]=I_{P}/B
,
and properties
(1),(2)
follow in a similar way.

∎

Let
j:\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}\hookrightarrow\textnormal{Sh}
and
i:\mathcal{F}l^{\sharp}_{y}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}l
.
If
(R,I,\gamma_{i})/W
is a ring with divided powers, define
\Omega^{1}_{(R,I)/W}

as the quotient of
\Omega^{1}_{R/W}
by the relations
d\gamma_{i}(x)=\gamma_{i-1}(x)dx
.
Define
T_{(R,I)/W}
as the dual of
\Omega^{1}_{(R,I)/W}
.
Then we define
T_{\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}}
and
T_{\mathcal{F}l^{\sharp}_{y}}

as the direct limit of
T_{(\mathcal{O}^{\sharp}_{\textnormal{Sh},x}/\mathfrak{m}^{n}_{x},\mathfrak{m}_{x})/W}

and
T_{(\mathcal{O}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{F}l,y}/\mathfrak{m}^{n}_{y},\mathfrak{m}_{x})/W}
. Using smoothness
and étale local coordinates
we see that
T_{\mathcal{F}l^{\sharp}_{y}}\cong i^{*}T_{\mathcal{F}l}
and
T_{\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}}\cong j^{*}T_{\textnormal{Sh}}
.

Proposition 2.7.3
.

Fix a trivialization
\phi
of
M_{0}
as before.
The differential of the Grothendieck–Messing map fits in the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\textnormal{Sh}^{\sharp}_{x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{P}^{*}T_{G/P^{\wedge}_{\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}F_{P}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d\pi_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}\textnormal{KS}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where the vertical isomorphism is induced from the isomorphism
\pi_{P}^{*}I_{P}\cong P_{\textnormal{dR}}

and
F_{G/P}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})\cong T_{G/P}
. In particular,

d\pi_{P}
is an isomorphism. Similarly,
d\pi_{B}
is an isomorphism fitting in the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\mathcal{F}l^{\sharp}_{y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{B}^{*}T_{G/B^{\wedge}_{\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}F_{B}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{b}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}e^{1}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

with
e^{1}_{1}
from
2.6.7
.

Proof.

For the first diagram, since
j^{*}\textnormal{KS}=\textnormal{KS}_{R^{\sharp}_{G}}
and
\pi_{P}
are induced from
\textnormal{KS}_{\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)}

and
\pi_{B_{0}}
it is enough to prove the analogous proposition for
(B_{0},P_{0},\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda))

and
R^{\sharp}
.
Let
\{e_{i}:i=1,\ldots,n\}
be the basis of
M_{0}
induced by
\phi
, with

\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}\}
corresponding to the Hodge filtration
M^{1}_{0}
.
Then
M^{1}\subset M_{R^{\sharp}}
is given by a basis

\{\tilde{e}_{i}\}=\{e_{1}+\tau_{1,m+1}e_{m+1}+\ldots+\tau_{1,n}e_{n},\ldots,e_{m}+\tau_{1,m+1}e_{m+1}+\ldots+\tau_{1,n}e_{n}\}
, where
\tau_{i,j}\in R^{\sharp}
.
Similarly, let
x_{ij}
be the coordinates on
\textnormal{GL}(\Lambda)/P_{0}

parametrizing the one-step filtration. Then
\pi_{P_{0}}

sends
x_{ij}
to
\tau_{ij}
. Identifying

F_{P_{0}}(\mathfrak{gl}_{n}/\mathfrak{p}_{0})
with
\textnormal{Hom}_{R^{\sharp}}(M^{1},M/M^{1})

the vertical isomorphism sends
\partial x_{ij}
to the map
\xi
defined
by
\tilde{e}_{i}\mapsto\tilde{e}_{j}

and every other basis element to
0
. This is because under the isomorphism
\pi^{*}I_{P}\cong P_{\textnormal{dR}}

in the proof of
2.7.2

(y,\pi^{*}_{0}g)
with

g=\begin{pmatrix}\text{id}_{m}&0\\
(x_{ij})&\text{id}_{n-m}\end{pmatrix}

(
\pi^{*}_{P_{0}}g
denotes the specialization of
g
along
\pi_{P_{0}}
, i.e. replacing
x_{ij}
with
\tau_{ij}
)
is sent to the trivialization of
M_{R^{\sharp}}
given by the basis

\{\tilde{e}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{e}_{m},e_{m+1},\ldots,e_{n}\}
.
Since
\nabla(e_{i})=0
by
Lemma

2.7.1
we see
that
\textnormal{KS}(\partial\tau_{ij})=\xi
. The commutativity follows since
d\pi_{P_{0}}(\partial\tau_{ij})=\partial x_{ij}
.

For the second diagram, it is also enough to prove it for
B_{0}
. Using the convention that

e_{i}\in M_{0}
generates the graded
F^{n-i}_{\Lambda}/F^{n-i+1}_{\Lambda}
of
\Lambda
under
\phi
, let

F^{n-i}_{M}
be generated by
\{f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{i}\}

where
f_{i}=e_{i}+y_{i,i+1}e_{i+1}+\ldots+y_{i,n}e_{n}
, and
y_{ij}\in R^{\sharp}_{B_{0}}
.
Let
z_{ij}
be the analogous coordinates in
\textnormal{GL}_{n}/B_{0}
. Then
\pi_{B_{0}}
maps
z_{ij}
to
y_{ij}
.
A similar analysis to before shows that
the vertical isomorphism sends
\partial z_{ij}
to the tuple of elements in
\textnormal{Hom}(F^{k}_{M},M/F^{k}_{M})

given by
f_{i}\mapsto\overline{f_{j}}
, and every other basis element to
0
. Then since
\nabla(e_{i})=0
,

\textnormal{KS}_{R_{B_{0}}}(\partial y_{ij})
is given by the compatible system sending
f_{i}\to\overline{f}_{j}

and the rest of the basis elements to
0
. This proves the commutativity.

∎

We note that the same result holds over the special fiber of
\mathcal{F}l^{\sharp}_{x}
, even though it
is a highly non-reduced ring.

2.8.
Vanishing of coherent cohomology

For applications it will be crucial to have good vanishing results for mod
p
coherent cohomology.
Most of these results are not available in full generality, so we will state a general expected theorem as an
assumption, and then specify the available results. This expected theorem is closely related to Goldring–Koskivirta’s
cone conjecture, which we now state. We refer to
Section

3.3
for the definition of
\mathop{\text{$G$-{\tt Zip}}}\nolimits
.

Conjecture 2.8.1

(
[
22
]
)
.

Let
C_{\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}}
be the cone of weights
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
such that there exists an integer
n\geq 1
satisfying

\textnormal{H}^{0}(\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}^{\textnormal{tor}},\omega(n\lambda))\neq 0
. Let
C_{\text{Zip}}
be the cone of weights
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
such that there exists an integer
n\geq 1
satisfying

\textnormal{H}^{0}(\mathop{\text{$G$-{\tt Zip}}}\nolimits,\underline{W(n\lambda)})\neq 0
. Here
\underline{W(\lambda)}
is
the vector bundle on
\mathop{\text{$G$-{\tt Zip}}}\nolimits
such that its pullback to
Sh
is
\omega(\lambda)
.
Then
C_{\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}}=C_{\text{Zip}}
.

The surjectivity of the Zip map
\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}^{\textnormal{tor}}\to\mathop{\text{$G$-{\tt Zip}}}\nolimits
implies the inclusion
C_{\text{Zip}}\subseteq C_{\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}}
,
and the conjecture states that this is an equality. Since
C_{\text{Zip}}
can be studied with simpler group theoretic techniques
one can get estimates on
C_{\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}}
assuming this conjecture.

Assumption 2.8.2
.

Let
(G,X,K)
be a Shimura datum of Hodge type and
\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}
its associated Shimura variety.
Then for
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
.

(1)

There exists some integer
n\geq 0
depending only on
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that for any
i\geq 1

\textnormal{H}^{i}(\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}^{\textnormal{tor}},\omega(\lambda)^{\textnormal{sub}})=0

for all
\lambda\in X_{1}(T)
satisfying
\langle\lambda-2w_{0,M}\rho,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle>n

for all
\alpha\in\Delta
.

(2)

There exists an integer
m\geq 0
depending only on
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that the following is satisfied.
For all
\alpha\in\Delta
and
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
such that

\langle\lambda,\beta^{\vee}\rangle\leq 3p+m
for all
\beta\in\Delta\setminus\{\alpha\}
,
then

\textnormal{H}^{0}(\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}^{\textnormal{tor}},\omega(\lambda))\neq 0
implies that

\langle\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle>-m
.

The first point has to do with the ampleness of the line bundles
\mathcal{L}(\lambda)
over
\mathcal{F}l^{\textnormal{tor}}
,
by applying a type of Kodaira vanishing result. In fact, in all cases known the vanishing holds for
\lambda
in a larger
region than the one stated. More precisely, it holds for
\lambda+2w_{0,M}\rho

where
\lambda
lies in a certain cone (the ample cone) contained in the dominant cone.
The second point is related to the cone conjecture in an obvious way. The constant
3
appearing in its statement is rather arbitrary,
but it is one that makes our proof of
Theorem

4.4.7
work.
In fact in
[
26
, §3]
they prove that in many cases the cone conjecture
implies
2.8.2
(2), but we remark that the cone conjecture is a much finer result.

Theorem 2.8.3
.

Let
\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}
of Hodge type, then

(1)

Let
p>\textnormal{dim}(\mathcal{F}l)
.
Part
(1)
in
2.8.2
is proved in the Siegel case by
[
1
]

and in the case of compact unitary Shimura varieties over a CM field
F/F^{+}
such that
the primes of
F^{+}
above
p
split over
F
in
[
76
]
.

(2)

Part
(2)
in
2.8.2
is proved for the Siegel case in
[
45
]
,
for Hilbert-Blumenthal
Shimura varieties
[
15
]
, and
for
G_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}=\textnormal{GL}_{4,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
,
G_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}=\textnormal{GL}_{3,\mathbb{F}_{p}}
,
G_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}=GU(3)_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
,
and
G_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}=GU(4)_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
such that
\mu
has signature
(3,1)
in
[
22
]
and

[
25
]
.

(3)

Assume that
\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}
is of PEL type, that
p>\textnormal{dim}(\overline{\textnormal{Sh}})
, and
that
G^{\textnormal{der}}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}
is almost-simple.
For generic weights
\lambda\in C_{0}
in the lowest
alcove and
w\in W^{M}

we have that

\textnormal{H}^{\bullet}(\overline{\textnormal{Sh}}^{\textnormal{tor}},\omega^{\textnormal{sub}}(w\cdot\lambda)^{\vee})
vanishes above
degree
d-l(w)
, and
